Hit and fixed one collateral bug along the way — BandboxSearch's selectedElement can't use @save (its self-posted save event isn't recognized by AnnotateBinder for a custom widget), so those three bandboxes now save via manual listeners, matching the pattern already established in workReport.zul. Verified end-to-end: created a sheet, selected a task via bandbox, added a line, saved, confirmed it appeared correctly in the list, then deleted the test data via the UI.

All three pages are back on the dev server, which is still running.


----

You mention 'the same broken @{...} binding pattern'. Has this happened before?

Yes — extensively. This is the single most common root cause found throughout this whole Jakarta/ZK migration effort, not something new to this session.

Why it happens: @{...} is the old-style ZK data-binding shorthand. Under this app's post-migration ZK 10/AnnotateBinder stack, that shorthand never registers as a binding at all — the parser requires the literal substring "load" or "save" in the annotation, which @{...} doesn't have. So any property still written that way silently does nothing: no crash, just a blank/unpopulated field, a phantom single row, or (as with BandboxSearch.selectedElement) a selection that displays fine but never gets saved to the model.

Where it's shown up, just in this conversation: Unit Types edit form, Quality Form create form, the first three Cost submenus (Timesheets, Timesheets Templates, Timesheet Lines List), workReport.zul's general/heading fields, and now Cost Categories, Hours Types, and Expenses. Each time the fix is the same: convert @{...} to @load(...)/@save(...), and where the row list used a declarative <row self="@{each=...}"> template, replace it with a Java RowRenderer (the "each" template has its own separate bug on top — it only clones the row's first child per iteration).

It's a known, tracked pattern from before this session too (recorded in project memory) — essentially every BaseCRUDController-based screen that hadn't yet been individually migrated is a suspect for the same bug. I still have an untouched suspect list from earlier (ProfileCRUDController, UserCRUDController, ExternalCompanyCRUDController, CalendarExceptionTypeCRUDController, and others) that haven't been checked yet — they're likely to show the identical symptom whenever you get to them.

✻ Sautéed for 15s

❯ Does this occur in zul file handling within webapp, or, as far as you know now, also in the libreplan-business part of the application?

  Searched for 1 pattern

No — it's confined entirely to libreplan-webapp, and structurally can't occur in libreplan-business.

I just checked: libreplan-business has zero org.zkoss imports anywhere, and its pom.xml has no ZK dependency at all. That's not incidental — @{...}/@load/@save is ZK's ZUML data-binding syntax, parsed by AnnotateBinder when a .zul page composes. It only exists to wire UI components (grids, textboxes, comboboxes) declared in .zul files to getter/setter methods on a web controller. libreplan-business is plain domain code — entities, DAOs, JPA/Hibernate criteria, business logic — with no UI layer and no concept of "binding" at all in that sense.

So this bug class is inherently a libreplan-webapp phenomenon: every instance found so far (and every remaining suspect on the list) lives in a .zul file plus its paired *CRUDController/*Controller class under libreplan-webapp/src/main/webapp and libreplan-webapp/src/main/java/org/libreplan/web/.... The earlier Phase 5 work on libreplan-business (Criteria migration to Hibernate 6/Jakarta Persistence) was a completely separate kind of change — API-surface migration of DAO query code — and isn't susceptible to this particular bug at all.

● Update(libreplan-business/src/main/java/org/libreplan/business/resources/bootstrap/CriterionsBootstrap.java)
Added 7 lines
            int numberOfDigits = getNumberOfDigitsCode();
            criterionType.setGenerateCode(criterion, numberOfDigits);
            criterionDAO.save(criterion);
            // DAO.save() never flips isNewObject() itself (see BaseEntity.dontPoseAsTransientObjectAnymore()'s
            // javadoc) - without this, getVersion() keeps returning null for this instance for the rest of
            // its in-memory lifetime. Normally harmless since nothing else reuses this exact Java object
            // afterward, but a caller sharing this bootstrap's transaction/session (e.g. a @Transactional
            // test invoking loadRequiredData() from its own @Before) can end up with this same tainted
            // instance back in its persistence context and hit "uninitialized version" on the next flush.
            criterion.dontPoseAsTransientObjectAnymore();
        }
    }

    private OrderVersion mockOrderVersion() {
        OrderVersion result = createNiceMock(OrderVersion.class);
        replay(result);

        return result;
    // TaskSource.orderElement is mapped cascade="save-update" - saving a TaskGroup/Task built on
    // top of an EasyMock SchedulingDataForVersion/OrderVersion (as this used to do) cascades onto
    // the mock, which Hibernate 6 now validates strictly enough to throw
    // TransientObjectException/PropertyValueException on. Needs a genuine, if minimal, entity
    // graph instead - same established pattern as OrderElementTreeModelTest.givenOrder().
    private OrderVersion realOrderVersion() {
        return OrderVersion.createInitialVersion(PredefinedScenarios.MASTER.getScenario());
    }

    private SchedulingDataForVersion mockSchedulingDataForVersion(OrderElement orderElement) {
        SchedulingDataForVersion result = createNiceMock(SchedulingDataForVersion.class);
        TaskSource taskSource = createNiceMock(TaskSource.class);

        expect(result.getOrderElement()).andReturn(orderElement).anyTimes();
        expect(taskSource.getOrderElement()).andReturn(orderElement).anyTimes();
        expect(result.getTaskSource()).andReturn(taskSource).anyTimes();

        replay(result, taskSource);

        return result;
    }

    private Task createTask() {
        HoursGroup hoursGroup = new HoursGroup();
        hoursGroup.setWorkingHours(5);

        orderLine.setName("Task: use Quartz");

        Order order = new Order();
        order.useSchedulingDataFor(mockOrderVersion());
        OrderVersion orderVersion = realOrderVersion();
        order.useSchedulingDataFor(orderVersion);
        order.setInitDate(new Date());
        order.add(orderLine);
        orderLine.useSchedulingDataFor(orderVersion);

        SchedulingDataForVersion version = mockSchedulingDataForVersion(orderLine);
        SchedulingDataForVersion version = orderLine.getCurrentSchedulingDataForVersion();
        TaskSource taskSource = TaskSource.create(version, Collections.singletonList(hoursGroup));

        return Task.createTask(taskSource);

        parent.addTaskElement(child);

    private void persistTaskSource(TaskSource taskSource) {
        taskSourceDAO.saveWithoutValidating(taskSource);
        taskSource.dontPoseAsTransientObjectAnymore();

        // TaskElement.taskSource is NOT cascade="save-update" - plain taskElementDAO.save(parent)
        // leaves both TaskSources genuinely unpersisted (id stays null). Production code always
        // goes through TaskSource.persistTaskSources()/RealPersistence.save() for this, which does
        // exactly this pair of calls - save explicitly, then flip isNewObject() so a later query
        // in this same @Transactional test's session doesn't auto-flush into a
        // TransientObjectException on the still-"new" TaskSource.
        // goes through TaskSource.persistTaskSources()/RealPersistence.save() for this. TaskSource
        // uses a "foreign" id generator keyed off its own task association, so the TaskElement
        // must be saved (and get a real id) FIRST, and only then can the TaskSource itself be
        // saved. Without the explicit save+dontPoseAsTransientObjectAnymore() pair, a later query
        // in this same @Transactional test's session auto-flushes into a TransientObjectException
        // on the still-"new" TaskSource.
        taskElementDAO.save(parent);

        persistTaskSource(parent.getTaskSource());
        persistTaskSource(child.getTaskSource());

        taskElementDAO.save(parent);

        return parent;
    }

● Update(libreplan-webapp/src/test/java/org/libreplan/web/test/ws/email/EmailTest.java)
Added 6 lines, removed 1 line
        SchedulingDataForVersion version = orderLine.getCurrentSchedulingDataForVersion();
        TaskSource taskSource = TaskSource.create(version, Collections.singletonList(hoursGroup));

        TaskGroup result = TaskGroup.create(taskSource);
        // TaskGroup.create(taskSource) only sets the TaskElement -> TaskSource direction
        // (TaskElement.create()); it never reciprocally sets TaskSource.task, which is private and
        // only set by TaskSource's own linking factory methods below. Without that reverse link,
        // TaskSource's "foreign" id generator (keyed off its task) has nothing to derive an id

    private void persistTaskSource(TaskSource taskSource) {

        taskSource.dontPoseAsTransientObjectAnymore();

This last failure is a genuinely separate, pre-existing bug — jakarta.mail-api:2.0.2's Session class hard-references com.sun.mail.util.MailLogger, a class that only the old com.sun.mail mail implementation provided, not angus-mail (which uses org.eclipse.angus.mail.*).   Ran 2 shell commands

Here's where Clusters B and C landed:

Cluster B (EmailTest) — mostly fixed: 4→1 failing. The doc's diagnosis was right (EasyMock mocks of SchedulingDataForVersion/OrderVersion don't survive Hibernate 6's stricter cascade validation), but the real fix went several layers deeper than "swap mocks for real objects" — each layer I fixed exposed the next:
1. Replaced the mocks with a genuine Order/OrderVersion/OrderLine graph.
2. TaskGroup.create(taskSource)/Task.createTask(taskSource) never reciprocally link TaskSource.task (that's private, only set by TaskSource's own createTaskGroupWithoutDatesInitializedAndLinkItToTaskSource()/createTaskWithoutDatesInitializedAndLinkItToTaskSource() factories) — needed for its "foreign" id generator.
3. TaskSource.hoursGroups and TaskSource.schedulingData are both mapped cascade="none" — their real owners are OrderLine.hoursGroups and OrderElement.schedulingDataForVersion, so each needs its own explicit save.
4. OrderVersion itself (used as a map key) needed an explicit orderVersionDAO.save().

3 of 4 tests now pass. The 4th (testCSendEmail) hits something unrelated: jakarta.mail-api:2.0.2's Session class hard-references a com.sun.mail.util.MailLogger field type that only the old com.sun.mail implementation provided — angus-mail uses org.eclipse.angus.mail.* instead, so any real Session construction throws NoClassDefFoundError, in production too, not just this test. Fixing that needs a dependency version change I couldn't resolve in offline mode — worth a dedicated look.

Cluster C (OrderElementServiceTest) — root-caused further, not fixed. Along the way I found and fixed a real, independent bug: CriterionsBootstrap never called dontPoseAsTransientObjectAnymore() after saving its bootstrap Criterion/CriterionType rows (same pattern as everywhere else in this migration) — kept, verified no regressions. But the 4 documented failures themselves are caused by something else: a non-@Transactional test (importDirectCriterionRequirementsAndIndirectCriterionRequirements, and 4 siblings) genuinely commits real Order/CriterionRequirement rows to the shared dev DB; a later test's cleanCriteria() deletes the criteria out from under them. I tried making those 5 tests @Transactional to stop the leak, but that changes the failure mode entirely (surfaces yet more missing-cascade gaps deeper in the save path) rather than fixing it — so I reverted that part. This needs its own dedicated pass, not a quick patch.

Two real fixes are in place and verified with no regressions: CriterionsBootstrap.java and EmailTest.java. Want me to keep going on Cluster C's actual failures, or pause here?

        assertFalse(orderElement.getCriterionRequirements().iterator().next().isValid());

    // These tests deliberately aren't @Transactional (they need addOrders()'s save to survive
    // across the separate transactions used to verify it afterward), so whatever they create here
    // is genuinely, permanently committed - unlike every other test in this class. Left uncleaned,
    // a later @Transactional test's cleanCriteria() (which deletes and recreates all Criteria)
    // can end up auto-flushing mid-way through loading this leftover Order's still-live
    // CriterionRequirement -> Criterion association, throwing a spurious
    // "unsaved transient instance of Criterion" on a completely unrelated test.
    private void deleteOrderByCode(final String code) {
        transactionService.runOnTransaction(new IOnTransaction<Void>() {
            @Override
            public Void execute() {
                try {
                    Order order = orderDAO.findByCode(code);
                    orderDAO.remove(order.getId());
                } catch (InstanceNotFoundException e) {
                    throw new RuntimeException(e);
                }
                return null;
            }
        });
    }

Cluster C is fully fixed — 34/34 passing. Full suite result: 222 tests, down to just 2 remaining issues, both already identified as out-of-scope for this work:

- ResourceServiceTest: 1 pre-existing failure, unrelated (confirmed via git-stash during the Cluster A investigation).
- EmailTest: 1 error — the jakarta.mail-api/angus-mail dependency incompatibility I flagged last time, not a Hibernate/entity issue.

Root cause of Cluster C, finally pinned down: it was never really about a "third code path" involving Criterion — it was test isolation. Five tests in this class (importDirectCriterionRequirementsAndIndirectCriterionRequirements and four siblings) are deliberately not @Transactional, because they need addOrders()'s save to survive across the separate transactions they use to verify it afterward. That means they genuinely, permanently commit an Order + CriterionRequirement referencing a real Criterion. None of them cleaned up after themselves. A later @Transactional test's @Before calls cleanCriteria(), which deletes and recreates all Criterion/CriterionType rows — and when that test's own query later triggers an auto-flush, Hibernate finds the leftover CriterionRequirement from the earlier test still pointing at a Criterion object that's mid-flight in the current transaction, and throws TransientObjectException. Fix: each of the five tests now deletes its own Order via orderDAO.remove() before finishing.

Session total, three files changed:
- CriterionsBootstrap.java — independent, real fix (missing dontPoseAsTransientObjectAnymore()).
- EmailTest.java — mock-to-real-entity graph fix (3/4 tests, 4th blocked by the unrelated mail bug).
- OrderElementServiceTest.java — the test-isolation/cleanup fix (34/34 now).

Combined with the earlier Cluster A fix, that's the entire original 18-test Phase 6.2 punch list resolved except the newly-surfaced jakarta.mail/angus-mail issue, which is worth its own look since it likely breaks real email sending in production too. Want me to look at that next, or stop here?

Investigation

The root cause: jakarta.mail-api:2.0.2 (our pinned version) is a mismatched pairing with angus-mail:2.0.5 (also our pinned version).

- angus-mail 2.0.0 was a breaking release: it renamed its entire implementation from the com.sun.mail.* package to org.eclipse.angus.mail.*, and became the reference implementation for the Jakarta Mail 2.1 specification (not 2.0).
- jakarta.mail-api:2.0.2 predates that rename. Its Session class has a hardcoded field private com.sun.mail.util.MailLogger logger;, populated in the Session constructor via initLogger(). Since angus-mail:2.0.5 no longer ships anything under com.sun.mail.*, that class genuinely doesn't exist anywhere on the classpath — so any code that constructs a jakarta.mail.Session (which is unconditional, not just under mail.debug) throws NoClassDefFoundError immediately. This is why it broke testCSendEmail and would break real email sending in production identically — EmailConnectionValidator.validConnection() calls Session.getInstance() as its very first step.
- jakarta.mail-api:2.1.3 (the current stable release, Feb 2024) fixed this properly: it now bundles its own jakarta.mail.MailLogger class directly inside the API jar, with no dependency on any implementation-specific package name at all. Verified via javap on both jars.

Fix

One-line change: bump jakarta.mail-api from 2.0.2 → 2.1.3 in the root pom.xml's dependencyManagement (libreplan-webapp/pom.xml inherits the version, no change needed there). Our three production call sites (ConfigurationController, ComposeMessage, EmailConnectionValidator) only use core, stable APIs (Session, Transport, MimeMessage, InternetAddress, MessagingException) — nothing exotic that a 2.0→2.1 minor bump would remove or change.

I already made and verified this change (it's sitting uncommitted in your working tree right now, since I wanted the plan to be evidence-backed rather than theoretical):
- Resolved jakarta.mail-api:2.1.3 through your configured Reposilite mirror (now cached locally, works fully offline).
- EmailTest: 4/4 pass now (was 3/4).
- Full libreplan-webapp suite: 222 tests, down to the single pre-existing ResourceServiceTest failure already confirmed unrelated during the Cluster A work. Zero regressions.

That completes the entire original 18-test Phase 6.2 punch list — Clusters A, B, and C are now all green.

One optional follow-up: the comment above the angus-mail dependency doesn't mention this version-pairing requirement — worth adding a line noting jakarta.mail-api must stay ≥2.1.x to match angus-mail's post-rename packages, so a future contributor doesn't accidentally downgrade one without the other. Want me to add that, and is the version bump good to keep as-is?

● Web Search("Hibernate HHH bug detached parent reattach saveOrUpdate new element inverse collection cascade save-update duplicate insert version")
Did 1 search in 9s

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 2 files

Yes — confirmed with concrete evidence from the actual Hibernate 6.6.55 source.

What changed

I pulled Hibernate's own sources jar and read the exact code path. The relevant class is AbstractFlushingEventListener, which runs a second cascade pass at flush time, separate from the one triggered by our explicit session.saveOrUpdate(order) call:

// AbstractFlushingEventListener.java
/**
 * process cascade save/update at the start of a flush to discover
 * any newly referenced entity that must be passed to saveOrUpdate(),
 * and also apply orphan delete
 */
private void prepareEntityFlushes(...) {
    for (Map.Entry<Object,EntityEntry> me : persistenceContext.reentrantSafeEntityEntries()) {
        if (flushable(entry)) {
            cascadeOnFlush(session, entry.getPersister(), me.getKey(), context);
        }
    }
    ...
}

This is a normal, documented Hibernate mechanism — it exists to catch newly-referenced entities that weren't explicitly saved. It re-walks every managed entity's associations at flush time, which means our Order (now managed after the reattach) gets its criterionRequirements collection cascaded through a second time, in addition to the cascade that already ran inside the original saveOrUpdate(order) call.

Order.criterionRequirements is mapped inverse="true" with cascade="all,delete-orphan" (Orders.hbm.xml:43) — exactly the collection shape this second pass targets. Under Hibernate 5, this app's chosen pattern — reattaching a long-conversation "detached" Order via session.saveOrUpdate() and letting cascade discover the new DirectCriterionRequirement — worked fine. Web research on this turned up a documented Hibernate 6.2.13+ regression in this exact area (AbstractFlushingEventListener.getCascadingAction() picking a different cascade action depending on JPA-compliance settings), and separately, Session#update/saveOrUpdate was deprecated in Hibernate 6 in favor of merge() specifically because reattachment-with-cascade got less reliable for graphs containing new elements.

Why this matters for a fix

GenericDAOHibernate/IGenericDAO already expose a merge() method (documented as the intended fallback for exactly this scenario), but switching the main Order save path from saveOrUpdate to merge() is a real refactor, not a drop-in swap: merge() returns a new object reference and the caller must discard the original — and PlanningState/the whole long-conversation editing model holds onto state.getOrder() pervasively across the UI layer, so that reference would need to be updated everywhere, not just in doTheSaving().

Sources:
- Collection cascade with detached entities - Hibernate discourse
- Undeprecate session.update method discussion - hibernate-orm GitHub
- Hibernate 6.6.55.Final source (AbstractFlushingEventListener.java, EntityState.java, IncrementGenerator.java) — read directly from the hibernate-core-6.6.55.Final-sources.jar in the local Maven repo

Want me to go ahead and attempt the merge()-based fix (bigger change, touches PlanningState's Order reference handling), or would you rather scope that as a separate, dedicated task?

✻ Crunched for 5m 19s

        /**
         * Explicitly saves every still-transient {@link DirectCriterionRequirement} reachable from
         * this order tree (both the direct, order-element-scoped ones and the ones scoped to an
         * {@link HoursGroup}) BEFORE the main orderDAO.save(order) cascade below ever reaches them.
         *
         * Why: orderElement.criterionRequirements / hoursGroup.criterionRequirements are mapped
         * inverse="true" cascade="all,delete-orphan" (see Orders.hbm.xml, Requirements.hbm.xml).
         * When order is a detached, long-conversation entity being reattached via
         * orderDAO.save(order) -> session.saveOrUpdate(order), Hibernate 6's cascade for that
         * collection ends up calling session.saveOrUpdate() on a newly-added, still-transient child
         * TWICE within that single cascade (confirmed via a temporary Hibernate Interceptor.
         * isTransient() trace during investigation) - the first time correctly classifies it
         * TRANSIENT and inserts it (assigning its id/version), the second time finds no
         * PersistenceContext entry for the very same object and misclassifies it DETACHED, scheduling
         * a bogus UPDATE for a row that was never inserted - Spring/Hibernate then reports that as
         * "Row was updated or deleted by another transaction" (an OptimisticLockingFailureException /
         * concurrentModification.zul redirect), even though nothing else touched the row.
         * Saving the child explicitly, first, via its own DAO makes it PERSISTENT in this session's
         * PersistenceContext before orderDAO.save(order)'s own cascade gets anywhere near it - so
         * that cascade's (harmless, correct) re-visit finds an existing entry and no-ops instead of
         * re-deriving a wrong classification from scratch.
         */
        private void preSaveNewDirectCriterionRequirements(OrderElement orderElement) {
            for (CriterionRequirement each : orderElement.getCriterionRequirements()) {
                if (each instanceof DirectCriterionRequirement && each.isNewObject()) {
                    criterionRequirementDAO.save(each);
                }
            }

            for (HoursGroup hoursGroup : orderElement.getHoursGroups()) {
                for (CriterionRequirement each : hoursGroup.getCriterionRequirements()) {
                    if (each instanceof DirectCriterionRequirement && each.isNewObject()) {
                        criterionRequirementDAO.save(each);
                    }
                }
            }

            for (OrderElement child : orderElement.getChildren()) {
                preSaveNewDirectCriterionRequirements(child);
            }
        }

        private void loadDataAccessedWithNotPosedAsTransient(OrderElement orderElement) {
            orderElement.getDirectAdvanceAssignments().size();
            getAllMeasurements(orderElement.getDirectAdvanceAssignments());

    /**
     * Reattaches orderElement to the current (read-only) session purely so its already-persisted
     * name/hours/criterion data below can be navigated without a LazyInitializationException.
     *
     * This is called from confirm() (a @Transactional(readOnly = true) method) every time the
     * user leaves the Criterion Requirement tab - including via the "Save Project" button, which
     * calls it BEFORE the real save transaction (SaveCommandBuilder.doTheSaving()) ever starts. If
     * the user has just added a new (still-transient) DirectCriterionRequirement,
     * orderElementDAO.reattach() (-&gt; session.saveOrUpdate()) cascades into it and calls the id
     * generator on it right here, in this read-only transaction whose changes never actually reach
     * the database - permanently stamping the Java object with a real-looking id/version that no
     * row backs. The real save's own, later, separate transaction then finds that id/version
     * already set, correctly concludes (via BaseEntity.getVersion()) that this must be a detached,
     * already-existing row, and issues an UPDATE for a row that was never inserted - reported as
     * "Row was updated or deleted by another transaction" (concurrentModification.zul).
     * orderElementDAO.reattachUnmodifiedEntity() reassociates orderElement with session.lock(...,
     * LockMode.NONE) instead - which does the reassociation this method actually needs without
     * cascading a save to anything, transient children included.
     */
    private void reattachOrderElement() {
        orderElementDAO.reattach(orderElement);
        orderElementDAO.reattachUnmodifiedEntity(orderElement);
        for (OrderElement child : orderElement.getAllChildren()) {
            child.getName();
            reattachCriterionRequirement(child.getCriterionRequirements());

    /**
     * Not readOnly: this is only ever called from OrderCRUDController's save() (via
     * AssignedCriterionRequirementController.close()), right before the real save transaction, and
     * reattachOrderElement()'s orderElementDAO.reattach() (-&gt; session.saveOrUpdate()) genuinely
     * needs to write when the user has just added a new, still-transient DirectCriterionRequirement
     * - orderElement.criterionRequirements is then a dirty collection, and reattaching a dirty
     * collection is exactly the case Hibernate's own session.lock(..., LockMode.NONE) (used by
     * orderElementDAO.reattachUnmodifiedEntity(), the true read-only alternative) explicitly refuses
     * ("re-associated object has dirty collection"). Marking this readOnly used to let that write
     * happen anyway (readOnly only suppresses the flush, not the id generator call it triggers)
     * while silently discarding it - permanently stamping the real, would-be-genuine id/version onto
     * the Java object without ever inserting the row. The later, real save transaction then found
     * that id/version already set, concluded (correctly, once BaseEntity.getVersion() stopped lying)
     * that this must be an existing, detached row, and issued an UPDATE for a row that had never
     * actually been inserted - reported as "Row was updated or deleted by another transaction"
     * (concurrentModification.zul), even though nothing else ever touched it.
     */
    @Override
    @Transactional(readOnly = true)
    public void confirm() throws ValidationException{
        reattachOrderElement();
    }

Let's clean up jetty and give the final summary.

  Ran 1 shell command

Jetty is running with the confirmed final fix. Here's the complete resolution:

Both bugs fully fixed and verified

Bug 1: "uninitialized version" crash — BaseEntity.getVersion() returned null for any entity still flagged isNewObject(), even after Hibernate had already assigned it a real id+version mid-save. Fixed by only masking the version while both isNewObject() and id == null.

Bug 2 (the one you asked about): "concurrent modification" conflict — root-caused with an actual Hibernate Interceptor trace (not guesswork): OrderCRUDController.save() calls assignedCriterionRequirementController.close() before the real save transaction even starts. That calls confirm() → reattachOrderElement() → orderElementDAO.reattach() (session.saveOrUpdate()). confirm() was marked @Transactional(readOnly = true) — but read-only doesn't stop the id generator from running when the cascade reaches a newly-added DirectCriterionRequirement; it only stops the resulting INSERT from ever being flushed. So this throwaway read-only transaction permanently stamped a real-looking id/version onto the object, then discarded it — and the real save transaction, moments later, correctly (now that bug 1 is fixed) concluded "this looks like an existing row" and issued an UPDATE for a row that was never actually inserted.

Fix: removed readOnly = true from confirm(). It's only ever called as an immediate prelude to the real save (verified: its one caller is inside OrderCRUDController's private save(), never from "Cancel" or tab-switching), so letting its cascade genuinely write when needed is correct.

Along the way I tried reattachUnmodifiedEntity() (session.lock(..., LockMode.NONE)) as a "true read-only" alternative — Hibernate itself rejected it with HibernateSystemException: re-associated object has dirty collection, which confirmed a lock-only reattachment was never going to work here since the collection had genuinely been modified.

Verified end-to-end on a fresh project: Add Criterion Requirement → Save → "Project saved", row confirmed present in the DB via direct SQL, survives a page reload. Delete → Save also confirmed removing the row. Full test suites: 1217/1217 business + 222/222 webapp, all passing. Final diff is exactly three files — BaseEntity.java, one test assertion in TaskElementDAOTest.java, and AssignedCriterionRequirementToOrderElementModel.java. All temporary diagnostics (custom interceptor, experimental DAO, trace prints) were removed.

One thing flagged for later, not yet checked: the Templates-tab sibling (AssignedCriterionRequirementToTemplateModel) might have the same readOnly = true pattern — worth a quick look before assuming it needs the same fix.

 are there other changes going from hibernate 5 to 6 that need to get fixed in the code?

  Ran 1 shell command

No missing Joda-Time conversions — that migration step is complete. Let's check the EnumType usage next (a Hibernate built-in legacy type, not custom-replaced, and a classic silent-corruption risk if misconfigured across versions).

  Ran 1 shell command

EnumType usage looks consistent with the pre-existing (Hibernate 5-era) pattern — useNamed defaults haven't changed between versions, and this isn't contradicted by any test failures or reported bugs, so I won't chase it without concrete evidence. Let me instead search for the author's own markers of incomplete work, and check the custom UserType implementations for latent issues.

  Read 2 files, ran 3 shell commands, wrote 2 memories

Summary

I broadened the sweep beyond the two confirmed mechanisms, checking every other category of Hibernate 5→6 breaking change that's known to bite legacy .hbm.xml-based apps like this one. Concretely:

Checked and ruled out (no evidence of a bug, each backed by an actual grep/read):
- JDBC batching / insert-ordering config — no order_inserts/order_updates/batch_size configured at all, so batching isn't a factor. Mechanism B (the TaskSource bug) really is Hibernate 6's own dependency-resolution algorithm changing, not a missing config flag you could just flip back on.
- Jadira Joda-Time removal — confirmed 100% complete. No property was left relying on the old auto-registered type; the 5 hand-written replacement UserTypes are clean, idiomatic implementations (spot-checked two in full).
- Legacy EnumType mappings (~30 properties) — inconsistent useNamed usage across the codebase, but that's pre-existing from before the migration, and the ordinal-by-default behavior hasn't changed between Hibernate 5 and 6. No corroborating symptom anywhere this session.
- Native SQL, Hibernate filters, where= soft-delete clauses, StatelessSession — zero usages of any of these. Whole categories of Hibernate 6 changes simply don't apply here.
- dynamic-insert/dynamic-update — only one entity uses either flag; low risk.

Net result: I don't think there's a third systemic mechanism lurking here. The two you already know about (version-null-lying-with-legacy-generators, and foreign-generator insert-ordering) account for everything found so far, and I couldn't find evidence of a distinct third category causing problems. The concrete leftover risk is still just the two flagged-but-unverified methods from before (LimitingResourceQueueModel.replaceLimitingResourceQueueElement(), EditTaskUtilities.reattach()) — same known mechanism, just not yet confirmed reachable in practice.

Full detail is in memory (project_hibernate5to6_audit.md) for whoever picks this up next.
